This isn't dancefloor techno in the usual sense - it's built for reflection rather than raving and is a meditation this time on hype itself: how it works as a kind of psychological currency, how fleeting attention gets mistaken for lasting substance and how quickly people move from believer to revisionist, chasing that first rush again. True to the series' whole ethos, Mills treats electronic music here as a vehicle for storytelling across dense, thoughtful sounds meant for those with attention spans too many people seem to lack these days.

 Asha Puthli & Say She She - "Pawa!" (Dino Soccio extended Disco mix) (6:34)
 Review: Ragz and Turbitto's Naya Beat label has thus far excelled at blending old and new South Asian music with 21st century house, disco and Balearica. they once more invite admired producers and artist friends to rework tracks from the label's growing catalogue. The key cut here - at least in terms of sheer dancefloor potential - is Woolfy v Projections' fine revision of Ragz & Turbitto's 'Nanan Da Oyar De Gaya'. Sitting somewhere between psychedelic Indian electrofunk and Tom Tom Club's 'Wordyrappinghood', it's a stellar, funky and sub-heavy rub. Elsewhere, Turbitto and Ragz give hyped outfit Boys Shorts the Naya Beat makeover, Psychemagik man Thomas Banks turns Pinky Ann Rihal's 'Tru Asia' into a dark wave throbber and Dino Soccio provides a fine disco extension of Asha Pushli and Say She She's 'Pawa'. … Read more

 Fell Asleep In The Sun (3:56)
 Review: After is the stage name adopted by Los Angeles duo Justine Dorsey and Graham Epstein. They formed in 2023 after meeting on the dating app Hinge and within two years signed to Mom + Pop records (Courtney Barnett, Del Water Gap, Beach Bunny). They have two EPs to their name and now this debut album, which takes their Y2-K inspired trip-pop sound to the next level. 'Open Water' is the most overtly trip-hop number on here, with Dorsey sounding quite Portishead at times. 'Breathe Your Air' is a beautifully melodic number to luxuriate in. Meanwhile, the emotionally charged duet 'Blind' is refreshing in that it brings the more elusive Epstein's voice into the mix as they take turns on lines. A feel good entry all round. … Read more

 The Pavements Light (feat Haruomi Hosono)
 Review: Pioneering Aussie downtempo and alt-electronic dons The Avalanches' dig into the strange, magical pull of advertising and name-brand technology on our earliest memories with their new album, No Bad Memories. It is an ambivalent ode to commercial jingles and proprietary startup sounds and a comment on how tightly childhood and corporate media have become intertwined. Robbie Chater describes the album's sampling process as a journey of discovery and has likened it to feeling like a kid again as forgotten jingles and half-remembered sounds keep opening new doors. True to form, the group builds its foundations from these raw materials rather than simply referencing them and turns consumer nostalgia into something strange and beautiful. … Read more

 No Bad Memories

 The Pavements Light (feat Haruomi Hosono)
 Review: The past 30 years have taught us that the Avalanches are a genuinely unique proposition: sample-hunting cut-up merchants whose densely layered compositions are like nothing else around. Even so, few will have predicted No Bad Memories, a concept album inspired by the relationship between consumerism and children (and capitol's attempt to flog stuff to them). It's an interesting idea explored through thrill-a-minute cuts heavy on children's singing, lo-fi synth-pop sounds, inventive instrumentation choices, off-kilter hip-hop beats, 16-bit bleeps and snippets of cheesy old toy commercials. As ever with their work, it takes a few listens to really comprehend everything that's going on, but that's no bad thing - few others would even attempt such an exercise, let alone execute it as impressively. … Read more

 How Much I Care (3:40)
 Review: Under the Baril alias, Marc Holstege made his name with pleasingly tactile and evocative 2023 debut album For You, Forever, a set that did a good job in combining chillwave with deeply dreamy electronics and nods to breaks and classic downtempo grooves. Belated second album In Between Highs sees him shift his signature sound slightly, offering similarly dreamy, immersive and wide-eyed jams that draw more inspiration from the worlds of deep house and Balearica. It's tactile, soft-focus electronica with a dancefloor pulse, where musical beauty, atmospheric opacity and soul-enrichment take priority over peak-time grunt or sofa-ready relaxation. Most importantly of all, it's a cracking good listen. … Read more

 Window To Your Soul

 'Til The End Of Time
 Review: While best known for the humungous remixes of 'Silence' that came out around 99/2000 (and every bootleg and update that's ever followed), Delerium should be celebrated and acknowledged for so much more as they were part of the earliest electronic movements in their native Canada. An off-shoot of Front Line Assembly with roots in the legendary Skinny Puppy, Leeb and Fulber's downtempo project Delerium was hugely prolific from 86 onwards, releasing countless studio albums throughout the 90s and well into the 00s. This 97 release, however, is one of their most successful and perhaps most comprehensive as it catches their range and so many different strands of the era. The sultry, trippy pop Madonna was doing at the time ('Til The End Of Time', 'Duende'), the cosmic ambient, backroom sound ('Window To Your Soul', 'Twilight') and the more new age-facing elements that were still dominating chillout compilations at the time ('Enchanted', 'Window To Your Soul') are all strong elements in this evergreen album. A genuine time capsule that still sounds fantastic today. And yes, it has the original version of 'Silence' on it. … Read more

 Bloom (instrumental) (7:07)
 Review: By 2001, Suffolk-born Brian Eno had the odd problem of sounding like a reference point to his own imitators. Drawn From Life, made with German composer J Peter Schwalm, mostly dodges that trap through its string writing. 'Persis' circles a dark ostinato that keeps needling at the electronics, then Laurie Anderson's vocodered intonations make 'Like Pictures Part #2' feel slightly unreal. The beats can be almost comically polite but 'Bloom' gets the balance right. Elegant stuff, with just enough strangeness left in the seams. … Read more

 July 23
 Review: By the time they arrived at third album Black Market Gardening in 1996, Hull trip hop/house duo Fila Brazillia were approaching the peak of their powers. Comfortable in their position as one of the world's premier downtempo duos, Steve Cobby and Dave 'Man' McSherry set about exceeding the hype engulfing them from the more stoned corners of dance music culture. As this 30th anniversary vinyl reissue proves, the album remains an impressively strong set. For the most part, it's a near horizontal shuffle of immersive chords, dubby basslines, head-nodding jazz and hip-hop breaks, and flashes of the stoner funk sound they further developed ion 1997's Powerclown. Highlights include languid opener 'Obrigado', pitched-down jazz-funk jam 'Little Dipper', deep drum & bass workout 'Butter My Mask', Balearic dub disco jam 'Wigs, Biifocals & Nourishment' and wonderful ambient closer 'July 23'. … Read more

 Squaremode - "The Wheel" (original Piano reprise) (3:50)
 Review: A reissue of a reissue here! Released in 2016, the original Rebirth 10 was a 47-track, 2CD mix album put together by deep house founding father Larry 'Mr Fingers' Heard in honour of the Italian label's 10th anniversary. A double vinyl version featuring 16 of the 47 cuts in all their full-length glory was then put out for Record Store Day in 2024, and now that version is being rereleased in limited-edition, clear vinyl form to mark the label's 20th birthday. Deep house from the jazzier side is the order of the day generally (though Akra's 'Pure' does a very passable impression of the synthier Chi-town deepness with which Heard originally made his name), and with tracks and remixes from the likes of James Teej, Tevo Howard, Joe Claussell, Ripperton, Motor City Drum Ensemble, Jamie 3:36 and of course Heard himself, lovers of late-night grooves will find much to enjoy here.

 Rame Head (4:03)
 Review: Jon Tye has spent three decades making gentle, optimistic electronics in various projects from his 1990s group MLO to his Ocean Moon solo alias and his work running Lo Recordings. Silver Ley, formerly Bethany Ley, is a Bristol harpist and vocalist who has worked with Ishmael Ensemble, Batu and Alabaster De Plume, and Prismatic Fields is their first record together. Glossy harp glissandos, synth waves and enchanted vocals all drift through a fantasy-leaning dreamscape here that will bring to mind genre greats like Boards of Canada. It's functional music for calming minds and feels formless, as if it's always playing somewhere rather than having been created for this album. … Read more

 Review: Berlin-based Olsvanger has spent recent years popping up on labels such as Kalahari Oyster Cult, Hooj Choons and Craigie Knowes, and the Honey Tangerine album finds him rifling through another drawer of club oddities. 'In A Nutshell' bolts acidic figures onto a driving bassline before a euphoric vocal barges upstairs, then 'Tulpa Nine' slows the machinery to a smoky crawl. Best of all, 'Zoom It Out' lets chiming keys and breaky house drums jostle for elbow room. Six tracks, several changes of weather and, very little concern for staying in one lane. … Read more

 Review: For the uninitiated, Rotation Sound System is a collective of veteran crate diggers, DJs, producers and party promoters spearheaded by Mind Fair's Dean Meredith and Ben Shenton, and Wreckin' Havoc trio Rob J, Stuart Robinson and Rich Hall.  Illumination (5:32)
 Review: To mark the launch of their new Hawara International offshoot, Austrian imprint Editions Hawara have unearthed a genuine gem from the margins of Japanese synthesiser music - a hard-to-find solo set of ambient-meets-jazz fusion gorgeousness from Piper member Takashi Shima. It was original produced in 1984 as part of a series of albums (the 'Sun Tan Club' series) part funded by a sun-cream manufacturer. Designed as an accompaniment to "light tanning", it sees Shima combine field recordings of lapping waves with slowly shifting chords, jaunty melodies, and the kind of sunny and colourful synth sounds that not only recall the new age musical movement of the 1980s, but also early Japanese synth-ambient and now sought-after library music albums of the period. … Read more

 A Lonesome Sentiment (6:19)
 Review: Almost 20 years on, Washington-based Drew Sullivan's 2007 debut as Slow Dancing Society hasn't dated the way you'd expect. The Sound Of Lights When Dim is a reserved work with reverberating guitar drifting through broad, unhurried tones. On opener 'Be There' reversed guitar and blurred vocal traces dissolve before they resolve on 'The Delicate Sound Of Silence.' It's deeply emotional ambient with endless oceanic scale and plenty of encouragement to reflect on your own. The glowing synths, patient guitar work and preoccupation with memory that would define Sullivan's next two decades are all present from the start and still have the same lasting impact. … Read more

 Lonely Deckchair - "I Didn't Fall Down"
 Review: Two decades on, Is It Balearic? continue to ask a gloriously impossible-to-answer question while remaining one of the most trusted curators in the game and always underlining that Balearic is less a genre than a state of mind. Across this essential anniversary collection, founders Timm Sure and Ampo trace the label's journey through sun-drenched house, cosmic disco, dub excursions and blissful leftfield grooves with standouts from key artists including Coyote, Max Essa, Leo Mas, Chris Coco and Prins Thomas. Rather than a simple retrospective, the compilation celebrates two decades of impeccable taste while championing timeless music over fleeting trends. A fitting tribute to a label whose influence cannot really be overstated. … Read more

 Holding Jupiter (edit) (4:46)
 Review: Last seen delivering brilliant Balearic covers of classic rave cuts on the superb Dedications EP, Visions of Light (Simon Sheldon, Muzka and Dan Dub Lounge) return to NuNorthern Soul with sophomore album Mycelium Bloom. The trio specialises in a particularly Balearic take on ambient dub, as those who heard their fine 2025 debut LP The Mandela Vortex will attest. This time round, they've tweaked that signature sound a touch, incorporating hallucinatory electronics and more psychedelic sounds in tribute to the "magic" fungi referred to in the title. There's sun-soaked beauty and deep dub rhythms in spades, of course, but also a more trippy and intoxicating vibe that's hugely alluring. Impressive stuff all told. … Read more

 Recall At K
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1186766-01-01-11.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1186766-01-01-11.mp3] 
 Sadness Of Dreams
 Review: Justin Tripp is one half of the New York duo Georgia and he turns trip-hop in on itself on Heart of the Total Eclipse. His archives are famously vast, and he puts out unheard material every month on NTS's essential All Kind Music show.' Eerie, garbled vocals, skeletal rhythms and faded cabaret instrumentation make up an uncanny tapestry here. On 'Hill Climbing,' dusty drums push oscillations and feedback, and the "vocals" turn out to contain no words at all. 'Infrastructure of Meaninglessness' drifts through dream pop, while 'Fraction of Successes' leans fully into absurdity. It's a neo-noir soundtrack that questions how reliable memory is and takes you into a brilliantly illusory headspace. … Read more

 Strictly Necessary
 These cookies are required to deliver the service or functionality you request. They enable core features such as page navigation, secure access, authentication, and load balancing. Without them, the website cannot function properly. Consent is not required for these cookies.
 Performance
 These cookies collect information about how visitors use our website, such as page visits and traffic sources. This data helps us measure performance and improve our site and services. These cookies are only used with your consent.
 Affiliate and Marketing
 These cookies are set by our advertising partners to track browsing activity and build profiles based on your interests. This allows personalised advertising and remarketing on other websites. These cookies are only used with your consent.
